# Decision log

The register of every product and engineering decision for Accrued, one row per `DEC-nn`. The reasoning, options and owners live in [`docs/plan/09-decisions-adrs.md`](../plan/09-decisions-adrs.md) (section 9.3) and in chapter 01 section 1.9; this file records what was decided, when, and what binds the code.

## How to use this log

- **Find a decision.** Search this file by screen ID (for example `BND-01`), module or epic. The "blocks" column names what cannot be frozen until the row is decided.
- **Open rows.** Decide an open row in the session, taking the plan's proposed option (shown in the row), then change its status to `decided`, set the date, and name the artefact that binds the code. Do not wait for a meeting; `docs/plan/AMENDMENT-solo.md` item 7 applies.
- **New decisions.** Append a new `DEC-nn` row at the end, using the next free number. Never renumber or delete a row.
- **Artefact.** The ADR, SPEC, config file or task that makes the decision binding in code. "ADR for P-nn" means the ADR is not drafted yet; its number comes from [`docs/adr/backlog.md`](../adr/backlog.md).
- **Money.** Rows record the option, never an amount. Amounts are set in the vault.
- **Status.** `decided`, `open`, or `n/a, solo edition` (two-founder governance and the release train, voided by the solo amendment).

Dates are ISO. For open rows the date is the decide-by date from the plan.

## Register facts for DEC-01 to DEC-101 (F-02-T01; SPEC-046 AC-1, AC-2)

The plan's owner, effective decide-by and default-on-silence eligibility for the 101 register rows (DEC-01 to DEC-99 from chapter 09 §9.3, proposed DEC-100 and DEC-101 from chapter 01 §1.9). Where chapter 01 §1.9 lists a row, its owner and date win and chapter 09's date is kept in the note. Owners as written in the plan: **TF** is notanaskhan, **PF** is ayushkbhatia, **N** and **AY** likewise; under the solo amendment (`docs/plan/AMENDMENT-solo.md`) the builder holds every row, and a row marked "never defaults" stays open past its date until the builder records it. "Never defaults" covers SPEC-046 AC-2's list (DEC-03, DEC-17, DEC-22's commercial part, DEC-25, DEC-26, DEC-52, DEC-71, DEC-72, DEC-77, DEC-85), every row a red P-ADR records (`docs/adr/backlog.md`), and every row counsel must approve (chapter 09 §9.0). Status and artefact stay in the decisions table below. Generated from the plan on 2026-10-25 (DEC-364); a later row's facts come from its own entry.

Chapter 03 says organisations refreshes it on `engagement.status_changed`, but organisations can't read engagements (ADR-008, module direction), so a SECURITY DEFINER trigger on `engagements` recomputes it on every insert and every change of status, type or client, in the same transaction; the app role can't set it. So the import route declares the `STAFF` marker (staff issuer, with MFA) and no matrix row is added. The templates are platform-wide: `email_templates` (tenant_id NULL only, until R-01 adds per-firm overrides) and the insert-only `email_template_versions` are global tables with no app privilege. They are reached only through the definers `email_template_import` and `email_template_current`, in a new kernel `platform_transaction` that sets no tenant. | DEC-212 | The portal sign-in BFF core (P-08-T10) | Built on ADR-168. **No oracle:** every `start` answers the same 202 after the same floor (0.5 s), on any host; only a challenge for an active client membership with an active grant in the host's firm, where `portal.sign_in_v2` is on, emits `portal.sign_in_code_requested`. **The code is issued at delivery** (the invitation pattern): communications calls `issue_sign_in_code`, which keeps only a salted digest, then sends the published C16 template `portal.sign_in_code` in the firm's name (no template, nothing sent and a log line; import it per environment like `notification.mention`). **Limits:** 10-minute codes; per firm and email, every guess on any challenge or host is counted before it is compared (`portal_sign_in_attempt`, under row locks), so parallel requests and fresh challenges share the same three guesses in a 15-minute window, and the third wrong one locks for 15 minutes, unknown emails included; a start within 60 s of the last sends no second email; resend after 60 s, the old code void and the tries kept; start, resend and every refusal of verify take at least 0.5 s. A host no firm has active writes nothing. A token stops resolving once the person's grants are all revoked or expired; sign-out ends the token's own session even without the cookie, and a sign-in in the same browser ends the previous one. (Self-review found the first build compared before counting.) **Sessions:** `auth_sessions` gains the portal surface pinned to tenant and host; the access token is `pst_` plus 256 random bits, kept as SHA-256, 15 minutes; the refresh cookie `__Host-abacus-portal` rotates with reuse detection and needs `X-Abacus-CSRF: 1` and `Origin: https://<host>`. `current_context` resolves a portal token only on its own host; the tenant header can't choose another firm. **Spelling:** the contract's `portal.sign_in.*` audit names have two dots, which the unit of work refuses, so they are `portal_sign_in.*`. **Not yet:** the MFA floor (P-08-T17) — until it lands every verify signs in without a second step, so `portal.sign_in_v2` stays off in production; `/v1/me`'s portal half (P-08-T07) still takes only provider tokens; refusing a staff token on a portal host is P-08-T25's. | 2026-10-24 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/identity/portal_session.py`, `portal_auth_routes.py`; `communications/sign_in_codes.py`; migration 0116; TASK-999 | POR-01 | decided |

| DEC-217 | The word layer's space, cap and empty pages (P-03-T05) | **Space:** word boxes are page points with a top-left origin in the space of the server-rendered page image. pdfplumber and PDFium both apply the page's `/Rotate`, so C4 §3.3's "unrotated page space" means the client applies no rotation and only scales (`image_w / width_pt`); `evidence_pages.rotation` records the `/Rotate` value. **Cap:** the extractor's 200 pages and 5,000 words a page; text is the word's characters with control, format and private-use characters dropped, 200 characters at most. **Empty pages:** a page with no words gets an `evidence_pages` row with `has_text_layer=false` and no object (T13 reads it as `not_scannable`); a locked, damaged or non-PDF upload writes no rows. **Hostile geometry** (security review): a page side that is not finite, not positive or over 14,400 pt is unreadable: its row has no text layer and its geometry is clamped to 1–14,400 pt, so the insert never fails. **Failures never wedge:** a words step that fails after its retries leaves `upload_processing` complete (the backfill tries again), and the backfill counts and passes a source whose step fails (`BackfillInput.failed`). **Storage:** one sealed, content-addressed object per text page; migration 0119 adds `text_layer_size`, which storage needs to open an object. **Backfill:** `trust_layer_backfill:<tenant>` (background) walks PDF versions, then inbox files, by id cursor in batches of 50, only those an extraction could read, so a file that yields nothing is passed once. | 2026-10-24 | `evidence/words.py`; migration 0119; TASK-1004 | P-03-T06, T09, T13 | decided |
| DEC-218 | Citation v2 verification rules (P-03-T06) | **Registry:** `evidence.register_citable(kind, verifier, display)`, once per kind; evidence registers `document_text`, `sheet_cell` and `request_item` (verifier `v1`); any other kind is written unverified `locator_invalid` (verifier version `none`) until its owner registers one. `record_citations(tx, anchor=, claims=, run_id=)` verifies each claim inside the writer's unit of work, numbers the anchor's stack, writes one row and one `citation.created` audit event (ids only) each; the creator kind comes from the transaction's actor (agent rows name the run and inherit `app.on_behalf_of`). **Text rule:** NFKC, case-folded, invisible format and control characters dropped, whitespace collapsed. v2 requires the whole quote, its lines joined by a space, contiguously and on word boundaries (never inside a longer word or number); quotes over 2,000 characters are never searched (`quote_not_found`). The agents' legacy adapters keep SPEC-030's every-line-somewhere rule (`quote_rule=lines`) and the screener its exact rule and stored `quote_mismatch` code; v2 never uses the legacy rule (security review). **Authorisation:** every verifier first authorises the acting principal (`record_citations(tx, actor=…)`, which must be the transaction's actor) for `evidence.read` (or `request_item.read`) on the anchor's engagement; an anchor with no engagement, or a refusal, is `source_not_visible` before any source is read; a source that is missing or in another engagement is `source_not_found`, alike. **`document_text`:** a page the word layer lacks is `page_not_found`; a box with no word centres in it is `bbox_mismatch`; a quote must be a run of consecutive words inside the box (`bbox_mismatch` when it is elsewhere on the page, `quote_not_found` when it is not on it); the region becomes the quote's words, and `quote_sha256` is the SHA-256 of their normalised text. With no box the region is the quote's words; a quote on the page text in no run of words is verified with no region, its hash that of the matched normalised text (AC-3). A claim with no box, quote or value checks nothing and is `locator_invalid`; every verified `document_text` row carries a hash, a region or a printed value. A page without words in the layer (no layer yet, or text only PDFium reads) is verified on the extraction's page text with no region, and a box on such a page is `bbox_mismatch`. A cited value must be one of the region's printed amounts (`1,650.00`, `$1,650.00`, `(1,650.00)`, `1,650.00-`); alone, it lights the word that prints it; `value_text` is the source's printing, never the model's. A version nothing has read yet answers `page_not_found`. **`sheet_cell`:** the extraction's table (one sheet may go unnamed); quote contained after normalisation; value equal to the cell's amount. **`request_item`:** exists (`source_not_found`) and is in the anchor's engagement. **Masked projection:** verification reads the words as stored until P-03-T13 writes `pii_regions`; T13 switches the verifier to the masked words. | 2026-10-24 | `evidence/citations/`; TASK-1005 | P-03-T08, T10, T13 | decided |
| DEC-219 | Citation match-id grammar (P-03-T06) | C4 §3.1 freezes `ev:<version>#p<page>:<x0>,<top>,<x1>,<bottom>`, `<file>!<cell>` and `ms:<step_key>@<ver>`, but 0109's CHECK allowed only `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,80}`. Migration 0120 widens it to `[A-Za-z0-9._:#,!@-]{1,160}` (the contract wins; no rows existed). Coordinates are written to one decimal without trailing zeros (`72`, `84.3`); a document citation with no resolved region is `ev:<version>#p<page>`; `<file>` is the evidence version id, and a workbook's later sheets add `.s<index>` (`<version>.s2!C14`); request items are `req:<id>`. A `match_id` is written only on a verified row. | 2026-10-24 | migration 0120; `evidence/citations/verifiers.py`; TASK-1005 | P-03-T08, T09 | decided |
| DEC-220 | Follow-ups v2 (P-06-T14): storage, templates, recipients, the P-4 patch marker, the routes' flag, the screening-return send | **Storage (0121):** `follow_up_schedules` gains P-06 §4.2's columns. `body_sealed` holds the person's edited body, or the body as sent (frozen at send so the email is what they read); an unedited draft is composed from the template whenever it is read, so requests answered meanwhile drop out. `body_fingerprint` is the composed body's SHA-256 at drafting, `edit_fingerprint` the edit's. Undo clears the frozen body of an unedited draft. **Templates:** `notification.follow_up_sent` (Reminder) and `notification.follow_up_sent.screening_return` (Return), platform defaults imported by staff (C16); the C16 whitelist gains `firm_name` and the body-only block `request_list` (one request per line, refused in a subject); no template text is in code, and the SPEC-027 engagement routes compose from the same template (409 `follow_up_template_missing` until one is imported). **Drafting:** one draft per (engagement, client contact); a request's recipients are its client assignee, else the binder's current recipients who sign in to the portal, else the engagement's client admins; a request already waiting in a draft to that contact is never repeated; at most four per request and due date (POR-20). Template-composed drafts are `drafted_by_kind = 'agent'` (the C-31 "Drafted by Accrued Agent (Request Chaser)" marker), with the person who asked as the audit actor; `kind` is `chase` for a person's request, `overdue` for the P-4 tick. The engagement need not be open again: its requests were already sent. **What is chased (security review):** only the open, client-visible requests of a **Published** binder; drafting over a binder the caller can read that is not Published answers 409 `binder_not_published`, and binder rows and records offer `follow_up.*` only while Published. **Scope check (security review):** every value a client or a person wrote is checked on its own, at draft and again at send: each request title, the person's added text (an edit, links left out) and the SPEC-027 note; only the fixed template text and the portal link are exempt, as platform text. Request wording is not assumed to have been checked already (publish does not check it, and titles can be edited after sending). Fails closed; 409 `out_of_scope`, nothing written. **Send:** human only, grant-held for 10 s (ADR-161), the body frozen as sent, delivered by an agents subscriber on `follow_up.sent` through `communications.deliver_follow_up` (message ID = follow-up ID, so redelivery sends once). `follow_up.sent` joins the human-only register (0122). **The SPEC-027 engagement routes (security review, option b, kept):** `FollowUps.tsx` still uses them, so they are not retired; they now refuse a binder's draft (409 `follow_up_v2_only`), scope-check the titles and the note, and send through the same grant-held outbox path (`send_overdue`): no direct email after commit. **Concurrency (security review):** waiting drafts are read inside the unit of work, and a partial unique index allows one open `chase` draft per client contact per engagement; a concurrent request's requests join it (`ON CONFLICT`) unless it was edited. **allowed_actions:** state-aware: a waiting draft offers draft and send; a sent one offers send (Undo) to its sender inside the window only; a dismissed or delivered one offers nothing. `follow_up.draft` and `follow_up.send` require independence, as `request_item.send` does. **Open:** the human-only guard refuses `agent` and `policy` actors only; refusing a `system` actor for `follow_up.sent` needs a per-action rule in the 0097 trigger and its drift test (cross-cutting; a follow-up). | DEC-231 | The portal auth client (P-08-T11) | Built on ADR-168 and DEC-212, as the staff client (P-01-T11). **Memory only:** `app-core/auth/bff.ts` holds the opaque token and its expiry in module memory; nothing goes to web storage, a URL or a log, and a reload refreshes from the `__Host-abacus-portal` cookie. **Refresh:** `POST /v1/portal/auth/refresh` with `X-Abacus-CSRF: 1` (the browser adds the cookie and its own Origin), 30 s before expiry or on a 401; one in flight for every caller, and a 401 for a token older than the one held reuses the newer token instead of refreshing again, so the rotating cookie is never presented twice (reuse ends the session). **Once:** a 401 replays a request without a body once with the new token; a second 401 on the replay goes to sign-in without another refresh; a request with a body is not replayed (its caller retries). **Return:** a failed refresh goes to `/sign-in?return=<path and query>` (the screen contract's `?return=`, not `return_to`), never the fragment, once per sign-in; only a same-origin path that is not `/sign-in` or `/v1/` is returned to, and it wins over the role landing. **Pre-session:** `/v1/portal/auth/*` is never refreshed or replayed (a wrong code's 401 is POR-01's), and start, verify, resend and refresh carry no bearer; sign-out carries it and forgets the token even when the call fails. **Fixtures:** `refresh` stands for the persona's cookie (a token for a persona with a portal shell, else 401), so a fixture area takes the same path as live instead of skipping the session check. **CSP:** the portal build keeps the shared `connect-src 'self'` policy. **Not yet:** no sign-out control in the portal (the account menu is a later screen); the MFA second step (P-08-T17, T19). | 2026-10-24 | `apps/web/src/app-core/auth/bff.ts`, `routes/portal/index.tsx`, `domain/portal/sign-in.ts`; TASK-1016 | POR-01; POR-02 | decided |

| DEC-215 | One content hash for reviewables (P-04-T14): RFC 8785 in-house, decimals as strings, which owners move now | `review.hashing` canonicalises under RFC 8785 without a dependency and hashes with SHA-256 (lowercase hex, what `sign_offs.content_hash` holds). | DEC-216 | Matrix target conditions (P-04-T15): `not_preparer_of_target` and `reserved_role` | **Facts:** identity never imports review, so the owning service puts the target's facts on the resource (`Resource.target`, `TargetFacts`: preparers, reserved role, reserved person), as `ItemFacts` does for client conditions; without them the condition denies. **Preparers** are the owner's, from its own records, never the request (`ReviewableFacts.preparer_user_ids`: a workpaper's cell setters and citation adders; a trial balance's version adopters and annotators; a mapping set's creator and editors), plus everyone who has signed L1 on the target on any content (an earlier hash included). | DEC-232 | Security review fixes on the S6 clients and engagements rows (P-07-T10, P-07-T16) | **Reveal cap:** at most 20 TIN reveals per person per firm in a rolling hour, counted from `tin_reveals` under a per-person transaction lock so parallel calls cannot pass it. A preparer working a household or a notice reveals a handful; twenty covers a busy afternoon of e-file corrections and bounds a stolen session to twenty TINs an hour. Past it: 429 `tin_reveal_rate_limited` with facts `limit` and `retry_after` (seconds until the oldest reveal in the window ages out) and a `Retry-After` header (new kernel `DomainRateLimited`, routes may declare 429); a `tin.reveal_throttled` audit event in its own unit of work (the audit trail is the firm's security record; there is no separate security-alert hook yet), a warning log naming the tenant, person, entity and reason, and a counter by reason (the person stays out of metric labels: cardinality). Never the TIN. **Sealing:** `seal_tin` and `clear_tin` take the person's context and authorise `client.update` (intake passes `client.intake`) on the entity's client, after `visible_clients`, before anything is read or written: a walled or unknown entity is 404, so a conflict cannot cross a wall. `tin_conflict` carries no facts. Only a unique violation on `client_entities_one_tin` reads as a conflict; any other integrity failure (a CHECK) is raised as it is. **Step-up:** when a role allows the reveal but MFA is missing or stale, the attribute-layer denial is kept and answered 403 `mfa_step_up_required` (a coded `DomainForbidden`), so the UI can step up; a role, relationship or condition (offshore) denial stays a plain 403. **Drafts:** a draft that names an entity is always checked against the entity's own client: the person's walls are read first (`walled_from`, because `authorise` does not wall a firm-level client resource), then `engagement.create` on that client; walled or unknown is 404, also when reading, validating or creating a saved draft after a wall goes up. The G1 lookup is limited to the client so checked. **Open (identity):** `authorise`'s wall layer returns early for any resource without an engagement, so `Resource.client(...)` is not walled despite its docstring; every caller here reads walls first, but identity should wall a client resource itself. | 2026-10-24 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/organisations/tin.py`; `backend/src/abacus/modules/engagements/drafts.py`; `backend/src/abacus/kernel/errors.py`; `backend/src/abacus/api/app.py`; `tests/integration/test_tin_reveal.py`; `tests/integration/test_engagement_drafts.py`; TASK-1009, TASK-1011 | CLI-02, ENG-03, P-07-T13, P-07-T14, P-07-T42 | decided |

| DEC-250 | The portal MFA floor and second step (P-08-T17) | Built on ADR-168 ("the WorkOS TOTP, when the client's role needs MFA") and DEC-34. **Floor:** `identity.register_mfa_requirement(name, predicate)`; `mfa_required(tenant, user)` is true when the firm's policy covers every client user or any predicate is true, and **fails closed** when a predicate raises or takes over 2 s, when a floor predicate (`owner_grant`, identity: an active `owner` grant; `engagement_designation`, engagements: `approver_of_record`, `signing_officer` or `ske_designee` on any engagement, for the person's contacts, linked by `contacts.user_id` or named by any of their grants' `contact_id`) is unregistered, or when the policy can't be read. T-01 registers the TRI predicate; until then a taxpayer such as Claire Feld is not asked. **Stricter only:** `portal_security_policies` (tenant, RLS; app read-only until G-02 adds `portal_security.manage` and the writer) holds `mfa_for_all_client_users` (only widens) and `trusted_device_days` (0 to 30, only shortens; 0 never trusts a device). **Second step:** a right code for a covered person uses the challenge and answers `mfa_required` (factor enrolled) or `mfa_setup_required` (none) with a 5-minute single-use `mfa_token` kept as SHA-256 on `sign_in_challenges`; no session until `/mfa` or `/mfa/setup/verify` succeeds, and that session carries `mfa_at`. Second-step guesses (TOTP or backup code, setup's first code too) are counted before they are compared, under the same row locks as codes, in the person's own counter on `mfa_factors` (one factor, so one count across every firm) that a right email code does not reset; the third wrong one locks the factor and sets the firm's 15-minute `locked_until` for the email (`423 mfa_locked`; that firm's sign-in is locked too) and voids the token. **Setup:** only for a person with no enrolled factor, once per token (the secret is returned once and never stored); the factor's ids sit in a fourth global table, `mfa_factors` (the epic named three; the factor's provider ids need a home), one per person across firms (ADR-002), its issuer the firm's name at enrolment; an abandoned setup's pending factor is removed at WorkOS on the next setup. The first right code enrols it and returns **8 backup codes once**: 16 Crockford base32 characters (80 bits) in four groups, stored as SHA-256 (no salt: 80 random bits), each used once atomically, all replaced by a new enrolment. **Trusted device:** "trust this device" sets `__Host-abacus-portal-device` (HttpOnly, Secure, Strict) for the firm's days; it skips the second step only for an enrolled person in that firm while unrevoked, unexpired and younger than the firm's *current* days; the session it starts has no `mfa_at`. **Revocation:** identity consumes `access.changed` and `client_access.revoked`: the person's live portal sessions in the firm end (`revoked`) and their devices there are revoked, audited as `session.ended` and `portal_device.revoked`; a grant being added ends them too, and designating a contact `approver_of_record`, `signing_officer` or `ske_designee` emits `access.changed` for the people the contact names, so a raised floor applies at once. **Audit** (one dot, DEC-212): `portal_mfa.enrolled`, `.verified`, `.failed`, `.locked`, `portal_backup_code.used`, `portal_device.trusted`, `portal_device.revoked`. **Errors:** 401 `mfa_mismatch {tries_left}`, 423 `mfa_locked {locked_until}`, 410 `mfa_expired`, 503 `mfa_unavailable`. `/v1/me.mfa` is filled for a client membership (`trusted_until` null). (The self-review found the designation predicate matched only `contacts.user_id`, which nothing writes; a designation that didn't end existing sessions; a per-firm guess count on a shared factor; and a revocation committed apart from its audit rows. All fixed.) **Tests:** T10's sign-in fixture person is now a `contributor` (an owner needs the second step, covered in `test_portal_mfa.py`). **Not yet:** Tess Moreno's factor is not seeded (WorkOS makes the secret and it is never stored, so a synthetic factor can't be reproduced by a journey; her first staging sign-in enrols, and P-08-T26 decides j12 2b's fixture); unlock and MFA reset clearing `mfa_factors`, backup codes and devices (T21, which uses `revoked_reason = 'mfa_reset'`); IP and device in audit rows (DEC-60, as T10); the session's `mfa_at` is stored but not yet carried into the portal `AuthContext`, so `mfa_recent` rules still deny portal users; a TOTP reused within its 30-second step relies on WorkOS to refuse it (the in-memory fake accepts it); a first enrolment by whoever holds the email code is trust on first use (an enrolment notice email is a follow-up); WorkOS TOTP (MFA API) must be enabled per environment before `portal.sign_in_v2` goes on in production. | 2026-10-24 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/identity/{mfa_floor,portal_mfa,portal_session,portal_auth_routes}.py`, `engagements/contacts.py`; migration 0133; TASK-1040 | POR-01 | decided |

| DEC-262 | Record-mode phases and the EBP profile (A-01-T05) | **Phase to gate** (blueprint §3.3.2: each gate is what the next phase waits on): Fieldwork follows G4, Completion G5, Ready to release G6 and G7, Released G8, Archived G9; In review has no gate and is always recorded. A phase leaves record mode when any of its gates has a registered provider (`engagements.api.gate_registered`): recording it is 409 `phase_follows_gate`, and C1's `gate.met` derives it once all its gates are met (mode `derived`, actor `system`, `gate_ref`). Because SPEC-314 already computes G6 to G9 globally, Ready to release, Released and Archived are never recorded today; only Fieldwork and In review are, until A-04 registers G4 (the plan's interim record-mode G8/G9 meet SPEC-314's providers; A-01-T23 decides how they coexist). **Order:** forward one step; at or before the current phase is `phase_backward`, past the next is `phase_skip`; Completion is not offered until DF-05 (DV-12), so In review is followed by Ready to release. **Projection** stays on `assurance_work_items` (engagements owns the table; the assurance service writes through `engagements.api.move_assurance_phase` and `set_assurance_profile`); the 0068 `phase` CHECK already holds the registry codes and is kept, `archiving` and `archived` included. **Human only:** matrix denies agent and system, the service refuses any non-human or support context, and the table refuses a `recorded` row without a human actor and a `derived` row without the system and a gate. **Reason** optional, ≤ 300 characters, sealed under the tenant key. **Audit and outbox:** `assurance.phase_recorded` / `assurance.phase_derived` / `assurance.profile_updated` on the engagement target with identifier-only references; outbox `assurance.phase_changed` (engagement, work item, from, to, mode). **Profile:** plan kinds `dc`, `403b`, `db` only (no health and welfare plan before the BAA chain), `limited_scope_103a3c` boolean, `version` for 409 `stale_version` **Security review (2026-10-24):** (1) `assurance.phase_recorded` joins the human-only register (verb decide; migration 0135 redefines `abacus_human_only_actions()` as 0132's 27 entries plus it, the downgrade restoring the 27), so the audit trigger refuses it from an agent or a policy. `assurance.phase_derived` stays off the list: it is written by the system from a computed gate's `gate.met`, or by the person whose own release or archive met G8/G9, and an agent never writes a phase event at all (the event table admits `human` and `system` actors only). (2) The event's `actor_kind`/`actor_user_id` must be the session's own (`app.actor_kind`, `app.actor_id`), as 0132 binds review decisions. (3) The app role holds no UPDATE on `phase`: the SECURITY DEFINER insert trigger `assurance_phase_event_apply` refuses a `from_phase` other than the current one, any move that is not forward and a recorded move of more than one step (In review to Ready to release is one step while Completion is not offered), then moves `phase`, `phase_source`, `phase_changed_at` and `version`; so every phase change has its event. SPEC-314's release and archive lock now move the phase through `assurance.phase.advance_by_gate` (one derived event naming G8 or G9 and the partner or manager), not a bare update. A derived row needs its gate and may be a person's or the system's. (4) Derivation walks one step at a time, one event per step, while the next phase's gates are all computed and met (a met G9 never moves Planning to Archived). Today only G1 to G3 emit `gate.met` and they open no phase, so the derived path is dormant; with SPEC-314 computing G6 and G7 globally, Ready to release is unreachable in record mode and by derivation until those gates emit `gate.met` (the SPEC-314 conflict is A-01-T23's; nothing here widens it). (5) The sealed reason is bound to the event's own id (`assurance.phase.reason_of` reopens it). (6) PATCH profile answers 422 for a null `limited_scope_103a3c` and for a body that changes nothing. | 2026-10-24 | `migrations/versions/0134_assurance_profile_phase.py`, `assurance/phase.py`; TASK-1103 | — | decided |

| DEC-275 | Invitations v2: the invitee's path, attribution and revocation (P-08-T18) | Built on ADR-168 §1 and ADR-146. **Path:** POR-01 d previews with `POST /v1/invitations/preview` (pre-session; on the invitation's own firm's host; padded to 0.5 s; each miss counted against a digest of the caller's address in `invitation_failures`, ten an hour answering every token 404 for 15 minutes, as 0025's acceptance does; the epic's 429 is folded into that same 404). "Accept and continue" calls `start` with `invitation_token`: a live invitation of the host's firm sent to the typed address makes its invitee eligible before any grant, and their user row is found (a client of the firm with that email) or made (`urn:abacus:portal-invitee`, no membership), so the code email, the counted guesses and the MFA step all work as for any client; any other token is exactly the ordinary start. `verify` is unchanged: its session authorises nothing while the person holds no grant (`portal_context` needs a membership and a grant). `accept` takes that bearer through `IDENTITY` (`current_invitee`; sign-up and the staff acceptance still take only the hosted identity). **One unit of work:** the client membership, the template's grants (through `client_access.grant_in_unit_of_work`, the one insert path `grant_client_access` also uses), `terms_accepted_at` (an invitation with terms needs the same `terms_version`), the invitation accepted and its token gone (single use), the contact linked and active (organisations, through an identity slot engagements registers), and the person's other portal sessions and trusted devices in the firm ended. **Authority and attribution:** the inviter's authority is checked when the invitation is created (`client_contact.invite`; a template only from a firm person, its scopes within the engagement, re-checked at acceptance); acceptance does not re-authorise `client_access.grant` (which needs the inviter's fresh MFA), and every row is the inviter's: grants and their events `by` the inviter, `client_invitation.accepted` and each `client_access.granted` by the system on behalf of the inviter (INV-5). **No `access.changed`:** acceptance ends the other sessions itself, because the relay's revocation would also end the session the person accepted with; `client_access.granted` is still emitted per grant. **MFA:** when the new grants bring the person under the floor and the session has no second step, the session ends and `accept` answers `mfa_required`/`mfa_setup_required` with a single-use token (a used challenge carries it, `portal_mfa_begin_session`), so T17's `/mfa` routes finish; | DEC-296 | The upload policy (DEC-58 (c); Q-03-T016; ED-018 to ED-021), decided by ayushkbhatia on 2026-10-24 | **Size:** 50 MB (DEC-58 c), replacing SPEC-020 AC-9's 25 MB. The edge body limit stays 50 MB for every other raw body (SPEC-050 AC-24) and lets the file routes (item upload, inbox, acceptance and letter files) run 1 MB past it, so the route, not the edge, answers an oversize file with 422 `upload_too_large`. **Type:** decided by sniffing the bytes, and the file name's extension must agree; the allowlist is PDF (`pdf`), JPEG (`jpg`, `jpeg`), PNG, HEIC/HEIF (`heic`, `heif`), TIFF (`tif`, `tiff`), GIF, CSV, XLSX and DOCX. Anything else is 422 `upload_type_not_allowed`, including HTML, SVG, JavaScript, text that does not parse as CSV, and macro-enabled or legacy Office (`.xlsm`, `.docm`, `.doc`, `.xls`; an OOXML package carrying a VBA project is macro-enabled whatever its name). Text is `text/csv` only when it is UTF-8, not markup, and its rows read strictly with `,` `;` tab or `|` with at least one row of two fields. The malware scan runs before the type check, so malware answers `file_refused` whatever it claims to be. **PDFs with active content** (`/JavaScript`, `/JS`, `/OpenAction` or `/AA` with a JavaScript or launch action, `/Launch`, embedded files; names decoded, FlateDecode object streams inflated to 16 MB): stored, never extracted (`not_extracted/active_content`), OCR'd, previewed (`none/active_content`), read for words, or served by the viewer (manifest, pages, regions and search answer 409 `active_content`); the upload audits `evidence_version.flagged` (`inbox_file.flagged` for the inbox) and the checks add a firm-audience `active_content` problem, as the `duplicate` check flags a file for a person. **HEIC** is accepted (DEC-58 c); no allowlisted dependency renders it, so it is stored, `not_extracted/format_not_supported`, preview `none/not_previewable` ("Preview not available yet") until P-11-T25. | DEC-297 | Detached-key storage as built (P-06-T18; ADR-162 §5, §6, §8; plan alias "ADR-137") | **Which uploads:** a client principal's own item upload (`ctx.client`, not on the client's behalf) is stored in detached-key mode; firm uploads, uploads on the client's behalf, connector retrievals, generated files and every inbox file stay content-addressed and unchanged. The key guard refuses changing a key's identity, clearing a retained key, and un-destroying one; the app role may update only `retained_at`; `evidence_key_destroy` (SECURITY DEFINER) is the only erase. | DEC-329 | Secure links and recovery as built (P-08-T21; SPEC-320 AC-14, AC-18 to AC-20) | Built on ADR-168, DEC-250, DEC-275/277 and DEC-303. **Links:** `secure_links` (communications, migration 0156): 256 random bits per email, kept as SHA-256 only, 14 days, single use, for one contact, with the address it was sent to (`recipient_email`, lower-cased) so a renewal can go there and nowhere else; `communications.api.issue_secure_link` returns the token once, `resolve_secure_link` reads live links server-side. **Sign-in with a link:** `start {link_token}` answers **404 `link_expired`** for every link that can't be used (unknown, malformed, another firm's, used, revoked, expired: one body, padded), so POR-01 e shows for all of them; the epic's "unknown token → a" would be an oracle on links, so it is not kept. A live link is used only when the typed address is its own; any other address leaves it unused and the start is the ordinary 202. **Renewal:** `POST /v1/portal/links/renew` is pre-session, always 202 `{accepted: true}` after the same floor, for any token on any host; a link of the host's firm that is not revoked (expired or used included: the person still needs a way in) gets a new row with the same contact, purpose, target and address, at most 3 per link per hour; its token is issued at delivery and sent from the C16 template `portal.link_renewed` to the original address even if the contact's field changed since. An invitation token renews an expired, pending invitation at most twice (`renewal_count`, a fresh 14 days, the old token void, the new one at delivery to the invitation's address; `client_invitation.renewed` by the system); revoked or accepted never. Nothing is renewed while the firm's sign-in is paused. Audit: `secure_link.issued`, `.renewed`, `.token_issued` (the contract's `portal.link.renewed` has two dots). **Unlock:** served by organisations (which owns the contact) on the contract's path `POST /v1/clients/{client_id}/contacts/{contact_id}/portal-unlock` beside CLI-04's `/v1/contacts/{id}/unlock`; both clear the firm's sign-in lock for where the person signs in (DEC-303) and now also the second-step lock on the person's factor, audited `contact.unlocked` naming the person (the contract's `portal_sign_in.unlocked`). **MFA reset:** `POST …/mfa-reset` (security admin, fresh MFA, `Idempotency-Key`, authorised before the replay lookup): the factor and backup codes are deleted (the factor is the person's, one across firms, so the next sign-in in any firm enrols again: POR-01 c′), the person's trusted devices in this firm revoked as `mfa_reset` and their portal sessions here ended, in one unit of work with `portal_mfa.reset`; the WorkOS factor is removed after the commit, best effort; the client is emailed from `portal.mfa_reset_notice`. Both definers act only on a client of the session's firm. A contact of another client or a walled one is 404. **Pause:** ADR-157's `portal_sign_in` control (0086's `portal_sign_in_disabled` renamed), read through the new `kernel.controls.control_active` (re-exported as `platform.api.control_active`; 10 s cache; `ai_pause` and `read_only` fail closed), checked by start, verify, resend and the second-step routes: 503 `sign_in_paused`; refresh and signed-in routes never ask, so sessions carry on; set by an operator with `set_platform_control`, whose `platform_control.changed` in the firm is the contract's `portal_sign_in.paused/resumed`. **Security review (2026-10-25), all fixed:** (1, high) the factor is the person's in every firm, so a firm may reset or unlock it only for a person with an **active** client membership here and **no active membership in any other firm** (read through the identity role, which sees every firm; the definer also counts their live portal sessions and trusted devices elsewhere, so a wrong flag can't strip it); otherwise the reset answers 409 `factor_shared` and leaves the factor and backup codes alone, while this firm's own trusted devices and portal sessions still end (cleanly separable: both are tenant rows), audited `portal_mfa.reset_refused`, no notice email; an unlock still clears the firm's sign-in lock and answers 409 `factor_shared` with `portal_mfa.unlock_refused` when the shared factor is locked. The 409's copy routes the firm to platform support or the person's own re-enrolment (POR-12); per-firm factors would need an ADR over DEC-250. (2) links die with access: organisations revokes a contact's links when their invitation is revoked or their address changes, and `client_access.revoked` revokes the person's contacts' links (`secure_link.revoked`; contacts have no delete path yet, the slot is there for it); a renewal needs the contact `active` or `locked` with a sign-in address still equal to `recipient_email`; a chain (`chain_started_at`, `chain_length`) ends after 5 renewals or 60 days from its first link; an `invitation` link defers to the invitation's own state and two-renewal cap. The renewal's checks and insert now run in the firm's tenant session (`portal_link_find` only finds), serialised per contact by an advisory lock. (3) per contact across all their links: 3 renewals an hour and 10 a day; the answer stays the same 202. (4) every `abacus_tools` connection goes through `abacus_tools.db_connect.connect` (the app's `database_tls` decision, from `ABACUS_ENVIRONMENT` and `ABACUS_DATABASE_CA_FILE`), and lint TLS-001 refuses `asyncpg.connect` or `create_pool` without `ssl=` in `abacus_tools`, except the local-only `stack.py` and `local/seed_dev.py`. (5) the sign-in pause trusts a last-known control state for at most 60 seconds after it was read, then counts as paused; with nothing ever read it counts as paused. (6) `tests/unit/quality/test_fresh_mfa_minimum.py` (protected) fixes 20 actions that must stay in the register with `mfa_recent: required`, including `contact.mfa_reset` and `security_role.assign` (which joins the register, group `access_control`). (7) `portal_mfa.reset` and `contact.unlocked` join the human-only register (YAML, generated module, and 0156's redefinition of `abacus_human_only_actions()` from 0135's 28 entries; the downgrade restores the 28). **Main-session review of b0d47dd9 (supersedes the factor parts of item 1 above, and the MFA-reset clause earlier in this row):** the 409 `factor_shared` told a firm that its contact belongs to another firm, an oracle on another tenant, so it is gone with its error code and the cross-firm membership lookup. A firm's **MFA reset never deletes the person's factor or backup codes**, in any case: it requires an active client membership here and ends the person's trusted devices (`mfa_reset`) and portal sessions **in this firm only**, so their next sign-in here asks for the second step again; it answers the same success shape `{client_id, contact_id, trusted_devices_revoked, sessions_ended, reset_at}` (no `mfa_enrolled`) whatever other firms exist, audited `portal_mfa.reset` with those counts, and the notice email goes. The factor itself is reset only by the person (their POR-12 re-enrolment and recovery flow) or platform support; a client who has lost their authenticator and backup codes is routed there. **Unlock** clears only the firm's sign-in lock and answers the same way; a second-factor lock on the person's factor is left and never mentioned (it lapses after 15 minutes). `portal_mfa_unlock` is dropped from 0156. **Not yet:** R-01's callers, the invitation email on secure links, the two C16 templates per environment, the operator CLI. | 2026-10-25 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/communications/{secure_links,secure_link_routes,sign_in_codes}.py`, `identity/{portal_recovery,portal_session,portal_auth_routes,slots}.py`, `organisations/{contacts,contacts_contract}.py`, `kernel/controls.py`; migration 0156; `tests/integration/test_portal_recovery.py`; TASK-1199 | POR-01, CLI-04, ADM-06, R-01, P-08-T24 | decided |

| DEC-333 | The portal PBC read model's rules (R-01-T07; POR-03, POR-04; DEC-240 left them open) | **Who reads what:** the binder picker needs a full-client grant (a third party, a K-1 recipient or a firm member has no documents: 404, as POR-03 says for Sam Ortiz); each binder is authorised for `portal_documents.read` on its work item (`scope_only`), and a binder appears when the person reads at least one of its requests. A binder is client-readable when published, marked done, or closed after it was published (closed and archived binders are read-only: no `allowed_actions`). Its requests are the client-visible ones `visible_items(request_item.read)` gives (a client admin every client-visible one, a contributor those assigned to them), never a draft, a deleted request or one with an open delegation (INV-4): a delegated request goes to its third party directly, so a client admin reads only the delegates' organisation names (`other_parties`, contacts with no organisation left out) and a contributor no note. A client admin may open a published binder with no row of theirs (POR-03 h); anyone else gets 404. **Words:** `firm_provided` reads Done (nothing to upload); `not_applicable` or a standing client Ignore reads Ignored; `returned` Returned; `uploaded` and `received` Done; else To do. A row is `provided_by_connection` when a file arrived through a connection (`retrieved`/`imported`, DEC-58 f). **Filters:** `requested` leaves out what the firm waived; `all` is every row; `uploaded_by_me` the rows with a file the person uploaded; `needs_follow_up` Returned rows and To do rows past due; the counts (`total`, `done_count`, `open_count` = To do + Returned, `sorted_count` = rule or agent links) are the binder's whatever the filter. **Layout:** audit binders (`pbc`, `third_party`) are folder bands by category; the others section cards by the request's area (`audit_area`) within its category; category and group codes fold the label (`Health & Medical` → `health_medical`); a category is `complete` when it has requests and every one is Done or Ignored. **Receipts:** a `rule` link nobody accepted is `rule_linked_pending`; an emailed file `emailed`; a client upload `uploaded` (the person's) or `uploaded_by_other_contact` (first name only); anything the firm added `received`. **Withdraw** shows only on a client's own detached-key upload nobody ever accepted (a client admin also on other client uploads, DEC-298 OQ-5), with `evidence.withdrawal` on; withdrawn files are never listed, to anyone. **Returns:** the effective send-back's reason maps `wrong_period` to `wrong_year` on household binders (`organizer`, `intake`) and keeps `wrong_period` on the others; any other reason is `other`; its note and the reviewer's name are the firm's client-visible words (SPEC-031 Q4) and the returned file is struck. **POR-04:** guidance is the request's own firm-written `why` (SPEC-035's fixed copy when drafted guidance is not shown); last year's file is the newest live fulfilment of the same-titled request in the binder this one was rolled forward from, when the person reads it; pre-checks are the newest file's latest client-audience problem codes among the four the contract names. **Security review (2026-10-25):** (1) a closed binder is client-readable only if it was published when it closed (`published_at` set and no `unpublished_at` after it): a binder unpublished and then closed answers 404 everywhere (AC-7); (2) last year's binder takes the same rule and is authorised like any other (`portal_documents.read` on its work item) before its file name and date reach POR-04; (5) the `delegate` role (P-06's one-request delegation) opens no documents: the picker and every read answer 404 to it, as to a third party; (6) `comment_count` counts the request's own shared thread and the shared threads of its live files only (a withdrawn file's thread goes with it), and a category is named only when the client reads one of its requests or it holds none at all ("Nothing requested"), so a category of only internal-only or delegated requests is never shown; (7) an uploader is named only to the engagement's client people: a person with a client role on it by first name, a third party by its contact's organisation ("Harborline", e.g. for its upload on a request whose delegation has since ended), anyone else not at all (`by_first_name` null). | 2026-10-25 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/portal/{documents,documents_routes}.py`, `requests/portal_documents.py`, `evidence/portal_files.py`; | DEC-334 | The client's Undo of an Ignore and of Mark as Done (R-01-T08; DEC-24, ADR-161 clause 6, R-01 OQ-2 and R8) | **Window:** until the firm acts, not 10 seconds: ADR-161's 10-second window guards an effect that leaves the firm, and a client's Ignore or Mark as Done holds none (R-01 R8 names the difference; DEC-240 took OQ-2's option). The firm's acts that close it: for an Ignore, a waive, a receipt or a return of the request (its status leaves `requested`/`returned`, or `client_ignored_at` was cleared by `request_item.returned_to_client`); for Mark as Done, a request of the binder sent, waived or returned to the client after the mark (`request_item.sent`, `.waived`, `.returned_to_client`), or the binder no longer Complete. Then 409 `firm_already_acted` with the request's or binder's id. **Otherwise ADR-161 clause 6 holds:** a compensating event by the person who acted (another person is 403), nothing deleted (the Ignore's response, its audit event and the ignore note stay), once (a second Undo is 409 `nothing_to_undo`). The Ignore's Undo appends a `withdrawn` SPEC-042 response and clears `client_ignored_at`; Mark as Done's returns the binder to Published with its mark-done stamps cleared. Both audit and emit `request_item.ignore_undone` / `binder.mark_done_undone`, and are registered in the shared Undo suite with the window kind `until_firm_acts`. `request_item.ignore` requires a reason in the matrix; the Undo's is the code `undo`. **The note** is a shared, sealed client comment with origin `ignore_note` (migration 0157), audited `request_item.ignore_note_added` with the comment's id; it is no longer copied into `client_item_responses.note`. **Security review (2026-10-25):** (3) a request with an open delegation answers 404 to the client's Ignore and its Undo, checked under the request's row lock, which every delegation insert now takes too; (4) the Undo of Mark as Done locks every request row of the binder (a firm act in flight on one commits first and is then seen, or waits), and the firm's acts are found by an uncapped `action IN (…) AND effective_at > marked_done_at` query (`kernel.uow.audit_acted_since`); (8) an Ignore with a note also authorises `comment.create`, and a request's thread takes at most 3 ignore notes: a fourth Ignore with a note is 409 `ignore_notes_exhausted` with nothing written (comments are insert-only, so a note is never replaced), and an Ignore without one still works. **Deferred:** in-app kinds for the undos: nothing consumes `binder.marked_done` in-app yet, so there is no notice to withdraw; the kind lands with the notifications row for `binder.marked_done`. | 2026-10-25 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/requests/{client_undo,lifecycle,binders,comments,pbc_contract}.py`, migration 0157; `backend/tests/contracts/undo/test_undo_contract.py`; TASK-1205 | POR-03, POR-04, BND-02 | decided |

| DEC-321 | Moved, broken and relink as built (P-03-T12; SPEC-322 F6; ADR-166 §6; DEC-298, DEC-299) | **Moved:** a new version of an evidence item moves every citation to the item's earlier, live versions, with `cause_ref = evidence_version:<new>`; a citation already relinked is retired and never moves again (its successor row does). **Broken:** a withdrawn version's citations get `broken` (`withdrawn:<id>`); voided and erased sources follow when their events exist. **Withdrawn is as if it never arrived:** a withdrawn new version moves nothing; a `moved` event whose successor was withdrawn later no longer counts, so the citation reads `valid` again (state stays computed; nothing is updated). **Idempotent:** one `moved` or `broken` event per citation and cause (unique, migration 0152); audit and outbox only for rows written. **Delivery:** both detectors run inside evidence's existing handlers for `evidence_version.created` and `evidence_version.withdrawn` (in turn), so they run in the same relay cycle and the worker's subscription list is unchanged. **Relink:** a person in a live request; the successor is the item's newest live version; the old quote is the run of the old layer hashing to `quote_sha256` (or the stored value), searched on the successor (the cited page first) and verified there by the kind's verifier as the person; the new row keeps the anchor and `n`, with `supersedes_id`; a sheet cell keeps its sheet and cell; other kinds answer 422 `relink_unverified`. Owners of other versioned sources call `mark_sources_superseded` **Security review (2026-10-25):** `citation.relink` is authorised on the citing host, its engagement with its work item where it has one (walls, the archived rule, independence and work-item conditions apply there); the successor is searched only on its PII-scanned pages (`projection_of`, fail closed), so a `not_scannable` page is never searched and a relink onto an unscanned successor is 422 `relink_unverified`. | 2026-10-25 | `backend/src/abacus/modules/evidence/citations/{moved,display}.py`, `evidence/api.py`, `tests/integration/trust_kit/test_citations_moved.py` | SHL-07e | decided |
| DEC-322 | PII detection as built (P-03-T13; P-03 §4.4; SPEC-322 AC4.1, AC4.8; DEC-52, DEC-60, DEC-296, DEC-298) | **Anchoring:** a label anchors a candidate to its right on the same line, or on the next line down within three line heights and 160 pt of the label's columns (a form's box label sits above or left of its value). Labels are fixed phrases in code (TIN: social security number, SSN, ITIN, TIN, recipient's TIN, taxpayer identification number, identifying number; EIN: employer identification number, EIN, payer's TIN; account number; routing number, ABA; date of birth, DOB), golden-tested, never firm content. **Kinds:** `dd-ddddddd` or an EIN label alone is `ein`; a valid ITIN or an ITIN label `itin`; otherwise a TIN is `ssn`; nine digits under a routing label `routing_number`; an account (4 to 17 digits, dashes and a short letter prefix) under an account label `bank_account`; a date under a birth-date label `dob` (no last four). **Unlabelled:** SSN and ITIN forms only when valid, an EIN only with an assigned prefix, nine bare digits only as an ABA-valid routing number; amounts, dates and short numbers never. `fulfilment.voided` joins the human-only register (33 entries). **Reads:** `live_fulfilment()` leaves voided links out, so every request, binder, portal and screening read treats the link as never made. **Effects, in one unit of work:** the request returns to `requested` if nothing else fulfils it (`request_item.status_changed` emitted); the portal-drop inbox file waits again (status, assignment and decision cleared; `sort_outcome` keeps `rule_linked`, the sort's own record, so its once-only trigger stands); `fulfilment.voided` and `inbox_file.sorted` go to the outbox (the gate projector recomputes the inbox-match gate). **Where:** the route and the orchestration move to evidence (`drops_contract.py`, `rule_voids.py`), same path, action and models: the void touches the inbox file, and evidence already depends on requests (requests never imports evidence). **Refusals:** an unknown fulfilment of the caller's own engagement answers 409 `not_voidable` like any non-voidable one (it says nothing more); another firm's engagement answers 404. **Security review:** the void locks the request item before it checks for an accept, as an accept locks it, so the two never interleave; the version the link made is retired (`evidence_version_voids`, insert-only, bound by trigger to the person's own void) and `not_withdrawn()` and `withdrawn_among` leave it out, so evidence reads, duplicate checks and the screening and upload-review gates (which now hear `fulfilment.voided`) treat it as never arrived; the dropper's poll reads `waiting` once the link no longer stands and never shows a request a person later assigns; the database stamps `voided_at`; the idempotency key binds the engagement too. **For Ayush:** a request that was `returned` before the rule link goes to `requested` on the void (the epic's words), losing its send-back state; `fulfilment.confirm` carries no `independence: required`, unlike `evidence.accept` (a matrix change). **Not done here:** the screening aggregate of the request's batch recomposes on its next version event; a file back in the inbox is not re-offered to the matcher; the downgrade brings voided links back, so run it only before any void. | 2026-10-25 | `migrations/versions/0159_fulfilment_void.py`; `evidence/rule_voids.py`; links a person adds are left out of the scope check, as for every follow-up (DEC-220). | DEC-369 | Masked renditions and masked agent material as built (P-03-T15; SPEC-322 AC-24, AC-25, AC-30, AC-40; P-03 §4.4, §4.5; DEC-52, DEC-322) | **Burn:** in the isolated child, each detected box (page points scaled by the image over the word layer's page size, padded 2 px, rounded outward) is painted opaque in the page's background colour, which is the most common colour of the image's border. The page is RGB with no alpha. The rendition is `upload_previews` renderer `v2` with `mask_version` `m1`. **Workflow:** `upload_processing` v7 `patched("masked_preview")` after `pii_scan`; new runs skip the unmasked v3 preview (`patched("previews") and not patched("masked_preview")`). `trust_layer_backfill` v2 `patched("masks")`: sources with an extraction and no masked rendition get words, scan and rendition. **Fail closed:** a PDF rendition with pages but no scan run is never stored (`unscanned`; the backfill retries). A PDF whose word layer can't be read therefore has no served page. An image, a table or a `none` result is stored with no boxes; its pages read `not_scannable` and the viewer refuses them to an offshore reader (403). **Serving:** the viewer and the three legacy page routes serve only the masked rendition; they answer 409 `preview_outdated` while only a pre-masking preview exists. Legacy preview JSON reads the masked row when there is one. Its `page_lines` are kept only for scanned pages; a line crossing a region is rebuilt from the masked words inside it, and the kernel TIN scrub then runs on every line. Its sheets are masked. Offshore (plain 403) and walls (404) are unchanged. **Agent material:** the reviewer, the matcher and the entry assessor always read the masked projection, whoever the run acts for (the offshore-only switch is gone). A PDF with page text has no material until its scan. Sheets are masked cell by cell by code (`pii.masked_rows`). A cell is anchored by a label in its own text, by the nearest label cell to its left until a non-label value, or by the nearest labelled header above it. A column-anchored bank account must have at least 6 digits, so ledger account codes are kept. The kernel's TIN scrub runs on every page text and cell as a backstop. **Re-announcement:** because material now waits for the scan, `check_upload` emits `upload_checks.recorded` only when material can be built. The first scan of a document with page text re-announces it (`upload_checks.recorded` for a version with checks on a request item; `inbox_file.extracted` for a firm inbox file still waiting; a portal drop's sort keeps announcing its own). It does so only in a live v7 run: an upload with a pre-masking preview is never re-announced, so the backfill never re-runs agents on old uploads. **Verifier:** `document_text` reads a scanned page's masked words and `sheet_cell` masked sheets. A quote copied from masked text verifies; the clear value is `quote_not_found`. A page no scan covers verifies on the clear layer as before: no text leaves through a verdict, and agent material never includes such a page. The display and the moved detector re-read a quote on the masked words first and on the clear words second, which covers quotes verified before masking. **Security review fix round (2026-10-26; H1, H2, M1, L1, L2):** (H1) The word layer is now `w2`. It is in render space: the visible page box (MediaBox ∩ CropBox) with `/Rotate` applied and a top-left origin, which is exactly what PDFium renders. `pdf_layout.RenderSpace` maps pdfplumber's boxes back to user space and on to render space, so the burn only scales. Words are read from an unrotated copy, so text reads in order, and the detector runs in the content's upright frame (`pii.detect_on_page`) with boxes turned back into render space. The detector is now `d2`. Projection, viewer and masker read only `d2` scans and regions, so an earlier layer's boxes are never applied to `w2` words. (H2) Annotations and form fields are flattened into the content (PDFium `FPDFPage_Flatten`, `FLAT_NORMALDISPLAY`) before both word extraction and the masked render, and the render draws page content only (`draw_annots=False`). What is shown is what was scanned. Fail closed: a page has no text layer, and so reads `not_scannable` (refused to offshore readers), in any of these cases: its word boxes and PDFium's own text runs don't each overlap the other within 2 pt; its geometry can't be reconciled (render size against page box, or a MediaBox pdfplumber can't place); any letter or digit is set sideways, upside down or mirrored in the content; images cover 25% of the page or more (`IMAGE_SHARE`), which makes scans with an OCR layer, fax headers or Bates stamps not_scannable until P-11; it draws 40 or more Bézier segments (`CURVE_LIMIT`: outlined text; some chart and rounded-box pages also qualify); (L2) or its words pass `MAX_PAGE_WORDS`. Legacy `page_lines` are dropped on rotated pages, and elsewhere masked by text (every layer word inside a region is replaced by the region's token wherever the line prints it), never by the extraction's geometry. (M1) Sheets stay out of agent material for a client's portal drop and for a run acting for an offshore member (`sheets=False`), as DEC-322 and TASK-1207 had it. An onshore run gets cells masked by `pii.masked_rows`, where an unlabelled bank account or date of birth can still reach the model; that is outside SPEC-322's TIN containment. (L1) `UploadInput.announce` (default True, so recorded histories still decode) replaces the pre-masking-preview inference. The backfill's batch passes False, so a first scan in the backfill never re-announces an upload. **Deploy note:** runs in flight at deploy replay their old path, and every upload made before the deploy (and every `w1`/`d1` layer) has no masked rendition. Its pages answer 409 `preview_outdated` and its agent material is empty until `trust_layer_backfill` v2 runs (`python -m abacus_tools.trust_layer_backfill` per tenant, as an ECS one-off on staging and production after the deploy). **Second fix round (2026-10-26; N1, N2, L4, L5):** (N1) Image pixels can't be read before OCR (P-11). An image counts as unscanned content when it covers 0.25% of the page or more (`IMAGE_FLOOR`) and is big enough to hold a legible nine-digit number: 30 pt along and 6 pt across (`IMAGE_LONG`, `IMAGE_SHORT`). This catches a pasted SSN card, a W-2 photo, a cheque and most letterhead logos; marks under that size stay. Such a page keeps its words, so its text is still detected and masked for everyone, and agent material keeps the masked text (no agent consumes page images). Its scan lists it as both scanned and not scannable (`pii_scan_runs`; the word layer object carries `images`), so it reads `not_scannable` (`Projection.complete`) and is never served to a reader who may not read unmasked content. Decided over blanking images in an offshore-only rendition: that needs a second rendition per source and per-page storage. Accepted cost: offshore readers are refused pages carrying a logo above the floor. The 25% `IMAGE_SHARE` rule is gone, since image pages now keep their text masking. (N1 regression check) Two rules were too broad, and are tuned with fixtures, never loosened past a missed TIN: the upright rule now fails a page only for a run of nine digits (single spaces or hyphens allowed) among characters set sideways, upside down or mirrored. Sideways margin notes and a diagonal DRAFT watermark pass; a sideways TIN, spaced or not, fails. The 40-curve rule is now `outlined_text`: a row of nine or more glyph-sized (4–40 pt) curved paths of similar height on one baseline with glyph-sized gaps, or one top-level curved object of 20 or more Bézier segments that is glyph-tall and at least four times as wide. Rounded boxes and small vector logos pass; outlined digit rows fail. Every synthetic PDF generator in `abacus_tools.synthetic.documents` stays scannable with no image flag. (N2) `detect` also reads nine-digit spans of adjacent digit-only tokens on one line (gap at most 2 line heights: comb boxes, `900-70-` + `1234`, `900` `70` `1234`). It joins them as `ddd-dd-dddd`, or as `dd-ddddddd` when the first run has two digits, under the usual label and validity rules, with the region being the union of the boxes. After a TIN or EIN label, a trailing digit run and the next line's leading run that make nine digits are one value with a region on each line. The kernel scrub (`tin_patterns`) also reads nine digits broken three or more times by single spaces, tabs or hyphens (`9 0 0 7 0 1 2 3 4`), as any type by the validity rules. The shaped forms keep their own rules, so a dashed SSN form that fails them is still no EIN. (L4) Legacy `page_lines` match region words without their edge punctuation (NFKC). A page where any region's word isn't found as a token has no lines at all. (L5) The masked renderer is now `v3` and the mask `m2`; `masked_preview_for` serves only the current renderer version, so a `v2` rendition burned with the old geometry is never current. **Third fix round (2026-10-26; R1 to R5): every rule is now the broader fail-closed one.** Outlined text and sideways digits no longer drop a page's words. Like pictures, they mark the page `unread` (the word layer object's `unread`; the scan lists the page as scanned and as not scannable): its upright text is still detected and masked for everyone, and the page is never served to an offshore reader. (R1) An image counts when it could hold a nine-digit number at about 4 pt: 20 pt along and 4 pt across, with no area floor. Only hairlines and specks are left. (R2) Outlined text (`drawn_glyphs`) is any 9 or more top-level vector objects of glyph size (largest side 4 to 40 pt; straight or curved; overlapping or not) anywhere on the page. It also counts a form XObject holding 9 or more paths, or one glyph-tall object at least four times as wide with 20 or more segments. Small vector logos and glyph-sized form furniture therefore mark a page, while boxes bigger than a glyph don't. Accepted cost: more pages are refused to offshore readers. (R3) Any digit not set upright marks the page `unread`, so sideways margin notes with a revision date do too. Words set sideways, upside down or mirrored are left out of the layer, so their reversed text never reaches agents, search or quotes. Sideways letters alone (a DRAFT watermark) don't mark the page. (R4) The detector's digit pieces take hyphens, dashes (U+2010–2015, minus), dots, slashes, unmappable glyphs (`(cid:N)`) and lone separator tokens between them, as the scrub does. A single hyphenated or bare token keeps its own validity rules. Masks spread by 0.6 of their height on each side and a tenth above and below (`MASK_SPREAD`), because the two parsers can disagree on a glyph's width. Over-masking a neighbouring label is accepted. (R5) After a TIN or EIN label, a fragment of 2 or more digits that never makes nine is masked on its own, with its digits sealed and no last four shown. A fragment ending a page carries over: the next page's leading digit run is masked too (`pii.page_dangles`, `continued`). Lows: an EIN split by more than two line heights now has both pieces masked by the fragment rule. The scrub's over-masking of single-digit count rows is accepted. **Residual pixel risk for offshore readers until OCR (P-11):** a page is served to an offshore reader only when it holds no picture of 20 by 4 pt or more, no group of 9 glyph-sized vector shapes, no sideways digit, and its words reconcile with PDFium's text. Its pixels are then text the word layer read, with every detected value burned out. What can still show is text the detector does not recognise as PII: a TIN with no label and an invalid number (an SSN-shaped fictional value), a TIN broken by unusual characters or words between its digits, a number split in ways the run, wrap and fragment rules don't join, a value far from its label (beyond the line below), or a date of birth or account number outside the label phrases. Also: shapes made of fewer than 9 glyph-sized objects (such as the last four digits drawn as outlines), and content pdfplumber and PDFium both miss or both misplace. A masked-text-only view for offshore readers until P-11 would remove the pixel channel entirely; that is a product decision for the coordinator. **Offshore text-only view (decided by Ayush in chat, 2026-10-26: "Offshore readers get the masked-text-only view (no page images) until OCR (P-11) lands"):** A reader who may not read unmasked content (`identity.may_read_unmasked`: an offshore member, or a run acting for one) is never served page pixels or originals of any evidence version, whatever the scan found. They get 403 before anything is read: on the C4 page route `…/pages/{n}` (now refused for every page, not only unread ones); on the legacy upload, version and inbox preview pages and their preview JSON, the `…/content` download, the statement preview and the setup files (already refused since TASK-1207, unchanged). There are no thumbnails, and no other route returns evidence image or original bytes; sheets are never rendered as images. The firm export download and the reveal are `not_offshore` in the matrix (DEC-370 unchanged). Instead the manifest carries `view` (`images` or `text`, decided by the server) and they read `GET /v1/evidence-versions/{v}/text` (`ViewerTextOut`). That is the masked text projection agents get: each page's upright words with detected regions as tokens, then the kernel TIN scrub; `unread` per page for content no scan reads (that page still gives its upright masked words); a workbook's cells through `pii.masked_rows`. It answers `no-store`, is logged as a content read, and answers 409 `preview_outdated` before the PII scan. Citation quotes are already re-read from the masked layer, and `value_text` is withheld for such readers. Regions and search stay geometry-only, and the text view draws no highlights. SPA: SHL-08's EvidenceViewer and the cockpit dock render `app-core/masked-text.tsx` when the manifest says `text`, and never request page images or regions. New copy, pending the design file: "Page images aren't shown for your access. This is the document's text with personal data masked.", "No text on this page" and "Loading text…". `unread` reuses P-03 AC4.8's "Scanned page · personal data isn't detected automatically yet". The legacy screens (`EvidencePreview`, `SourceHover`) keep their existing refusal state for offshore readers. Onshore viewers and agent material are unchanged; the round-three page rules still decide `unread` and the onshore masks. **Deploy note:** offshore readers see the text view from deploy on, independent of the backfill. Before a version's backfill, its text view answers 409 `preview_outdated` (no scan yet) and its pages stay refused. **Final round (2026-10-26; M-A, M-B, low):** (M-A) A reader who may not read unmasked content gets each sheet in `…/text` as its name only (`withheld: true`, no rows), as their agents get no sheet. New copy: "This sheet isn't shown for your access.". (M-B) The scan joins an unlabelled TIN split by a page break (`pii.across_break`): the trailing digit run of one page's last line and the leading run of the next page's first line, when together they make nine digits that pass the unlabelled rules, are one value with a region on each page. Text, agent material and renditions therefore mask both pieces. A labelled split stays with `dangles`/`continued`. | DEC-434 | Audit log reads as built (G-02-T08, TASK-1329; SPEC-349 §4, §7, §9; DEC-420 L4 and the S9 security review's bindings) | **Firm-level events:** an event's engagement is its `engagement_id`, else its target when that is an engagement; an event with none is firm-level (sign-ins, security settings, walls, firm structure, unscoped history) and is shown only to readers allowed the new matrix row `audit_event.read_firm_level` at the firm: `firm_admin`, `security_admin`, platform support and the QM owner (`quality_partner`, whose `audit_event.read` cell is `in_scope` and names no engagement at the firm; DEC-420 already names it a request-context reader). Request context is likewise the row `audit_event.read_request_context` (the three `REQUEST_CONTEXT_READERS`): AUTHZ-002 keeps firm roles out of module code. Engagement roles never see a firm-level event, whatever their cell. **Engagement events** pass `visible(ctx, "audit_event.read", engagement)`, which applies roles, in-scope and the wall on the engagement's client: walls key on a client derived from `engagement_id`, for scoped events and for history whose only engagement is its target. A stored `client_id` the reader is walled from hides the event too. **Security review M1:** writers do not fill `audit_events.client_id` yet, so for a walled firm-level reader a firm-level event is also hidden when its `before_ref`/`after_ref` name a walled client (`client_id`, as `legal_hold.placed` does) or carry any other identifier outside `FIRM_SAFE_REFS` (people and firm structure), such as a contact, entity or engagement it cannot place (fail closed); list, detail, counts and search share the rule. **L5:** the three role-dependent rows are probed through `allowed_actions`, so an ordinary read logs no `authz.denied`. A firm-level reader walled from any client sees, among unscoped events, only `targets.FIRM_LEVEL` kinds: history the explorer cannot place fails closed (most writers do not pass `Scope` yet; back-filling `engagement_id` is the fix). All of it in SQL, so `total` and `counts` never count an omitted event. **AC suite 1, AC-4:** the suite compared Hannah's total with Felix's minus one, which assumed every reader sees firm-level events; under this binding Hannah, a manager, reads only her engagement's events, so the test now gives her Copperleaf and asserts she sees exactly its event, no Ashford event and no count of either (stricter, not weaker). **IP filter:** only for `REQUEST_CONTEXT_READERS`; for anyone else it matches nothing, so nobody can probe an address they cannot see. **Office filter:** the object's `office_id` only; the actor's membership office fallback (DEC-415 Q9) waits for an identity reader of an office's people. **Facts:** reference ids and counts only, SHA-256 fingerprints dropped (a fingerprint of a short value can be brute-forced); `ok` only for a person or a policy. **Kernel:** `kernel/uow/explorer.py` (new) exposes the Core `audit_events` table and the covering checkpoint's leaf version, as `audit_activity()` does, so the module names no kernel table in SQL; `content_hash` is the event's leaf at that version. **Catalogue v1:** G-02's own actions have entries; the other 360 audited literals render through a verb-based fallback and are listed in `activity_reviewed.py`, held there by a static check. | (a) the security review's bindings, fail closed | 2026-10-26 | `audit_trail/{explorer,audit_log_routes,activity_catalogue,activity_reviewed}.py`, `kernel/uow/explorer.py`, `docs/contracts/audit-log.md`; TASK-1329 | G-02-T13, T17, T21; A-01 (`events_for_work_item`) | decided |

| DEC-436 | Audit label index as built (G-02-T10, TASK-1331; migration 0192) | **Tables:** `audit_event_labels` (PK `(tenant_id, seq)`, RLS forced, GIN on `keywords`, the app inserts and updates, never deletes) and `audit_exports` for G-02-T17, in one migration as G-02 §4.2 plans. **Content:** actor label (the person, or the person an agent acted for), target label (owners' labelers, never above Confidential), the labels of people the references name, and the action's words; no identifier is indexed as text and no value is ever read, so an SSN in a client's name or reference is never findable. **Maintenance:** `index_batch` upserts after the newest indexed `seq` (idempotent on replay); `backfill` rewrites from the start. The 60 s `AuditLabelIndexer` Schedule is **not wired** (it would change the shared Schedule set and need recorded replay histories): until it is, every `q` search first catches the firm's index up by one batch of 2,000. **Writes:** operational, like the hash chain's checkpoints: no audit event (it would index itself) and their own commit, so UOW-001 excludes `audit_trail/labels.py`. **Search:** every word required (`plainto_tsquery`, `simple` config), applied as a predicate inside the explorer's own query, so visibility, the keyset page and the capped total apply to matches together (security review L3; no cap before visibility). **Late commits (M2):** `seq` commits out of order, so each catch-up batch also re-reads the last 120 s of events with no label (the chain's settle window) and upserts them. **One indexer per firm (L4):** a batch takes `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` on the firm and writes nothing when it is held; rows go in one multi-row upsert. **L6:** a test holds `labels.py` to writing `audit_event_labels` only, with no raw SQL. **Known gaps:** writers fill neither `audit_events.client_id` nor `office_id` yet, so the label rows' copies are null and the Office filter (object's office) matches nothing until callers pass them (G-02-T06 left them to callers); the actor-office fallback (DEC-415 Q9) also waits for an identity reader. **Not measured:** the 500 ms p95 at 50,000 events is a staging check. | (a) as the epic, with the deviations recorded here | 2026-10-26 | `migrations/versions/0192_audit_trail_read.py`, `audit_trail/{labels,registry}.py`, `abacus_tools/quality/banned_patterns.py`; TASK-1331 | G-02-T17, T21 | decided |
| DEC-432 | The security-center contract v1 stub (G-02-T11, TASK-1332; SPEC-349 §8; `docs/contracts/security-center.md`) | **Placement:** each stub lives in the module that will implement it, because identity may import no other module (`MODULE_DEPENDENCIES`): identity holds the overview, sign-in and portal policies, the SSO-exempt account, sessions, unlock, the support switch and the posture (`identity/security_center_contract.py`, T14); platform holds the incident read side and decisions beside Q-02's create route, and the safeguards program (`platform/security_center_contract.py`, T18, T20); evidence holds the export schedule (`evidence/firm_export_schedule_contract.py`, T19), mounted before Q-02's firm-export router, whose `POST /{export_id}/approve` would otherwise take `schedule/approve`.
