The inputs forbid extra fields, so no request can name MFA, the passwordless code, the portal MFA floor or the lockout (422 before any handler); the session bounds are not validated above, so a longer limit reaches the service's 422 `policy_loosening_refused` with `facts.field`. Saves carry the body `version` (409 `stale_version`), as firm-settings v1 does. **Incident decisions:** the first decider names the second (`second_decider_user_id`), so the drawer can read "Waiting for Margaret Okonkwo"; anyone else, the same person or the same seat answers 409 `decider_must_differ`, a decided incident 409 `already_decided`. **Export schedule:** v1 is fixed at monthly, the 1st, 02:00 in the firm's zone (G-02 §4.2); `GET` is `firm_export.read`, not `firm_export.schedule`, so a partner can read what she approves without a fresh second factor; a repeat approval answers 409 `approver_must_differ`. **Safeguards:** `annual_report_next_due_month` is a month (the handoff gives "Dec 2026"); control titles come from the imported Pub 4557 definitions; Export WISP pack is `security.manage` (fresh MFA), its link Restricted. **Left to the audit log, not the contract:** who designated the SSO-exempt account and when, and when each schedule approval was given. **Error code added:** `sso_exempt_needs_hardware_key` (409, AC-18). **Fixtures:** local interfaces mirroring the models until `make generate`; ADM-06 a to h plus `d_waiting`, `sessions`, `unlock` and the support states requested, granted and revoked; "Declined" waits for G-02-T14 (the support-session status has no declined yet); ADM-06 i and j stay G-03-T29's; retention has no fixture (imported record types). | (a) as the epic, with the deviations recorded here | 2026-10-26 | `identity/security_center_contract.py`, `platform/security_center_contract.py`, `evidence/firm_export_schedule_contract.py`, `docs/contracts/security-center.md`, `apps/web/src/fixtures/{seed,handlers}/security-center.ts`, `scenarios/ADM-06.ts`; TASK-1332 | G-02-T12, T14, T16, T18 to T20 | decided |
| DEC-433 | Engagement export create, Undo, reads and build as built (A-01-T18, TASK-1334; A-01-T19, TASK-1335; SPEC-344 AC-6 to AC-13; A-01 §4.5; migration 0191) | **Order:** a non-human, support or client principal is 403 first; the work item resolves through `engagements.api.work_item_engagement` and `export.create` is authorised on its engagement with the work item (404 behind a wall, 403 by role, independence, `offshore`, stale MFA) before the `audits.binder_export` flag; then 422 `export_kind_unsupported` when the work item has no assurance row (any non-audit kind, not only tax returns), and 422 `binder_not_in_work_item` for a scope binder that is not one of the work item's asked (published, complete or closed) PBC binders as the requester reads them (`requests.api.pbc_binders`, new); an empty scope takes every such binder and a work item with none is the same 422 (nothing to export). **Grant:** `issue_grant(export.create)` bound to the SHA-256 of the canonical `{export_id, work_item_id, kind, scope}`, `not_before` = `kernel.clock` now + 10 s, expiring 7 days later; the workflow recomputes the digest from the row and consumes it with the export id as consumer. The requested event is not grant-held: the workflow, not the relay, consumes the grant. **Idempotency:** `kernel.uow.idempotency` on route `engagement_export.create`; the stored answer is the export id only and a replay reads the export fresh; authorisation runs before the replay (ED-023's shape). **Undo:** the requester only, as a person, while `now < not_before`; anyone else or later is 409 `undo_window_closed`, a consumed grant 409 `effect_started`. The guard trigger repeats the window on `abacus_now()` and the requester rule, and only lets the status move forward (queued to packaging, undone or failed; packaging to ready or failed; ready to expired with a `void_reason`). **Schema beyond M2:** `engagement_exports` also checks that `undone` carries `undone_at` and `undone_by`, `failed` a `failure_code`, and `not_before > requested_at`; the history index adds `id DESC` for the keyset; `engagement_export_contents` gains an index on the version for T22's voiding. Both insert-only tables share `engagement_export_rows_immutable` (listed in `schema_check.IMMUTABLE_TABLES`). Audit target kind `engagement_export` joins evidence's owners. No row is added to the human-only register: the database CHECK `requested_by_kind = 'human'` and the matrix refuse agents, and T25 adds the I-AGENT-NEVER rows. **Build (A-01-T19):** **Workflow:** `engagement_export` v1, id `engagement_export:<tenant>:<export_id>`, reuse rejected, work class batch, started by the `engagement_export.requested` subscriber with the rest of the window from the business clock; activities `consume_grant` (retried while `GrantNotYetDue`; a refused grant with the export still queued marks it failed `build_error`), `snapshot`, `package` (heartbeats; `integrity_mismatch` and `export_section_missing` are non-retryable), `mark_ready`, and `mark_failed` (unlimited retries). Audit events beyond §4.7, as the system on behalf of the requester: `engagement_export.started` and `engagement_export.snapshotted`; `engagement_export.failed` carries no code in its reference (codes are text; the row holds it). **Snapshot:** `as_of_seq` is the tenant's newest audit sequence when the snapshot runs, and the selected versions are written to `engagement_export_contents` then; the package keeps only those versions, so a file accepted later is not added and one withdrawn later drops out. Index and provenance rows are read when the package is built (no historical reads exist). **generated_at** is the export's `not_before`, so every build of one export is byte-identical. **Selection:** as the requester (`member_context`) through `requests.api.source_documents` per scope binder (categories numbered across binders in binder order; requests within a category in the order they were asked, never-asked last), files from live fulfilments with their effective decision (`accept` in; undecided only with `include_unaccepted`; rejected and sent back never). Paths: segments NFC, `/ \ :` and control or format characters replaced by `_`, whitespace collapsed, leading and trailing dots and spaces trimmed, TIN forms masked (`scrub_tins`, which masks through `kernel.classification.mask`), category 36, request 56 and file 60 characters, the file shortened further to stay within 180, duplicates compared without case suffixed `~2`. **Sections:** requests may not import evidence, so evidence registers `index`, `fulfilments`, `review_decisions` and `citations` itself from its reads of `requests.api`; agents registers `screening` (codes and identifiers, no rationale). Provenance carries identifiers and codes only: review notes, citation quotes and values, and screening rationales stay out. Status cells use the vocabulary's request labels without placeholders. Cells: TIN forms masked, a leading `= + - @`, tab or CR prefixed with an apostrophe in CSV, and the XLSX cell a string with `quotePrefix`. **Audit trail:** audit_trail may import only identity, engagements and platform, so evidence owns the slot `register_export_audit_trail(reader)` and the composition root registers `audit_trail.api.events_for_work_item`; with no reader a build that includes the trail fails `export_section_missing`. Each line: seq, effective_at, action, actor kind and ids, target type and id. **Store:** a new `exports_bucket` setting (unset: fails closed); segments `exports/<tenant>/<export_id>/segment-NNNN` of 64 MiB, each sealed with `kernel.crypto.seal` bound to `engagement_export:<export_id>:<n>`, no Object Lock; in `evidence/export_storage.py`, not `kernel/storage.py`, which stays the boto3 client factory (STORE-001) and may not import a module. The other steps' problems are 422 `draft_incomplete` first. **Duplicates:** a new client whose normalised name is a client the person may see is 409 `possible_duplicate` (pick the existing client); an entity name the client already has is 409 `entity_exists`. **New client:** status `prospect`. **Acceptance:** with `start_acceptance`, one record per engagement, kind `new_engagement_existing_client` under an existing client and `new_client` otherwise (`open_record` takes the kind); the answer names the first. **Relationships** run from the new entity to an entity of a client the person may see (walled or unknown 404), effective from the date of death when there is one; **contacts'** roles are scoped to the new entity. **Replay:** the route stores the answer by `Idempotency-Key`; the draft also keeps it (`result`, identifiers only), so a committed draft answers the same to any later key and writes nothing. **Audit:** `client_intake.started`, `.saved` (version only), `.discarded` on the draft; `client_intake.committed` on the client with identifier references; plus the writes' own events. **Security review (2026-10-26, changes requested; fixed):** H1, a check on a `client_intake_draft` searches the names the server builds from the draft (the typed client and entity names, each contact's name, and the names of the existing client and related entities, read by organisations through `register_conflict_subject_names`), never the caller's `names`; a draft with no names is 422 `invalid`. | DEC-425 | Password unlock as built (P-03-T19, TASK-1320; SPEC-322 F5; C4 §3.5; ADR-004, ADR-052, ADR-110) | **One child:** `unlock.derive_unlocked` runs decrypt (PDFium), the word layer (`words.extract_words`, w2), the PII scan (`pii.scan_layer`, d2, now shared with the upload activity so the two cannot drift) and the masker (`previews.render_masked`, v3/m2) in one isolated child with the default limits; the decrypted bytes never leave it and any exception becomes an outcome. **Derived records:** one `evidence_derivations` row and the pages, scan run, sealed regions and masked rendition under it, in the request's unit of work; the version's bytes and fingerprint are unchanged. Repository readers take `derivation_id` (default None keeps every existing caller on non-derived rows); only the viewer (manifest, pages, text, search) and the reveal read the version's current derivation. **No derived extraction yet:** no `upload_extractions` row is written, so agents keep reading the original's material, which an encrypted file never has (no unmasked text can reach them), and `evidence_version.unlocked` re-runs no checks yet (OQ-8's code-check re-run is a follow-up). **Limit:** five failed attempts per person per version in 15 minutes, counted over `evidence_version.unlock_failed` audit rows (`occurred_at`, system time) by a new kernel helper `audit_actor_count_since` under a per-(person, version) advisory lock held across the child run; the sixth answers 429 `unlock_rate_limited` (`limit`, `retry_after`) and is audited `evidence_version.unlock_throttled`. **Re-unlock:** a version with a derivation answers 409 `not_password_protected` and is no longer offered `evidence.unlock`. **Human only:** `serving_request()`, no support session or client principal, the matrix, and `created_by_user_id NOT NULL`; `evidence_version.unlocked` is not added to the human-only audit list (it needs a migration and is not an I-AGENT-NEVER verb). **Route:** keeps 501 among its declared errors, as the served reveal does, so the C4 stub-convention test holds. **Synthetic:** `password_pdf(password)` writes a real RC4 (R2) standard-security PDF with no dependency. **Security review fixes (2026-10-26):** M1, the older `POST …/request-items/{item}/uploads/{version}/unlock` (TASK-323, still used by the legacy firm and client upload views) now runs through the same guard (`unlock._attempt`: the per-(person, version) lock and `unlock_failed` counter), so both routes share one budget, and refuses agents, the system, support sessions and calls outside a request (`_live_person`; a client principal may still unlock their own upload there); it declares 429. The online limit only stops casual guessing: `GET …/content` lets any `evidence.read` holder take the encrypted file offline, where nothing limits guessing. M2, the isolated child sets `RLIMIT_CORE` to 0 (and on Linux `PR_SET_DUMPABLE` 0 via ctypes), so a crash leaves no core file with decrypted bytes or a password. L1, migration 0173 adds the partial unique index `evidence_derivations_one_unlock` (one `password_unlock` derivation per version); a concurrent second unlock answers 409 `not_password_protected`. **Follow-ups, not changed:** L2, an attempt holds a pooled connection (the advisory lock's transaction) for the child run, up to its 30 s timeout; L3, `evidence_version.unlocked` is not on the human-only audit list, which needs a register migration that would collide with this wave's 0175 list. |

Rows carry `bulk_request_id` = uuid5(tenant, signer, key) as a second guard. | DEC-507 | Idempotency keys are claimed, not locked (P-04-T22 security re-review M1; P-36; migration 0214) | The advisory lock that serialised a key held an idle connection for the whole request and could exhaust the pool. Instead `kernel.uow.idempotency` claims the key with a pending row (no status, no response) in one short committed upsert before the effect runs, and fills it after; nothing is held open while the effect runs. Another request finding the row: a different hash is 422 `idempotency_key_reused`; the same hash still pending is 409 `idempotency_in_progress` (new code); a filled row replays. A run that raises releases its claim (expires the row). **Claims are tokens (third round, re-review M1, M2, L1):** each claim writes a fresh `claim_id`, and only that claim fills or releases the row; a fill that touches no row (the claim was taken over) is logged `idempotency.claim_lost` and the caller still gets its own answer. A run releases its claim only if it failed before any unit of work inside it reached its commit (the UoW marks a watching caller before it sends COMMIT); after a commit the claim stays pending and a retry gets 409 rather than a second effect. The release is shielded from cancellation. **Stale claims:** a pending row of the same request older than 3600 s (`PENDING_RECLAIM_S`, far above the slowest keyed effect; a crash mid-run) and any expired row are taken over by the next claim, so a key never blocks forever; a stale pending row of another request is still 422. A BEFORE UPDATE trigger (search path pinned) lets the app only fill a pending row once, release its own pending claim, or take over an expired or stale row: a completed answer is never rewritten. The 3600 s also lives in that trigger; change both together. Lookups stay keyed by tenant (row-level security) and actor. `idempotency_keys` loses its insert-only status in schema-check: the app may UPDATE `request_sha256`, `claim_id`, `status_code`, `response`, `created_at`, `expires_at` (as the trigger allows), never DELETE. | 2026-10-27 | `backend/src/abacus/kernel/uow/idempotency.py`; migration 0214 | none | decided |

Uploads and request events are not scoped to the work item in the trail, so freshness is computed from the selection's own targets, not from a work-item scope. The table's insert trigger re-checks kind, status, expiry and manifest under the same lock. **Read-only:** C1's `archive_work_item` sets `work_items.archived_at` (never `archive_locked_at`) and archives the engagement once every work item is, so `authorise` refuses every write (the lever SPEC-314's lock uses). **Residual (for A-07):** a write that read the engagement as active before the archive committed can still commit after it; only A-07's database immutability closes that.
